- **2026-11-24 -- The Tasks kit knows its behaviors by their traits, assumes no one task by default, and writes only through the features' actions (`Kits/Tasks`, its sample, `Features/Panels`, `Features/Placement`).**
  - **Roles, not names**: the kit's core named verbs (`teleport`, `pause`, `advance`; the pairings check/checklist, quiz/option, a marker's next teleport, a fade's return); a project's own verb now takes each role by declaring it. New on `TaskBehaviorAttribute`: `Teleports` (its rows bring the user to a viewpoint, placed first where the row names none), `GoesOn` (its step goes on by itself), `Returns` (it sends its targets home), `Partner` (the behavior its rows pair with, either side naming the other: `TaskBehaviors.Pairs`). New interface `IStepGate` (a button its step waits on: `pause`). New: `TaskBehaviors.With(traits)`, `Pairs`, `Gate`. A manifest that turns off a behavior that `GoesOn` stops every step going on by itself, as turning off `advance` did.
  - **Defaults that assume no task** (`TaskSettings`): `debugTools` false (a shipped task carried a Skip button); `rig` is `Auto` (new `TaskRig.Auto`: a scene whose main camera is part of a rig of its own is left as it is, with a note, `TaskKit.RigCode`; any other gets the desktop rig); `anchorPattern` empty (`^UI_` hid a project's own `UI_` meshes); the placard and a step's page only where a step has something to show (new `pages`, `PlacardPages.Needed` / `EveryStep`; `TaskPage.Shows`, `OnPlacard`). The Tasks sample keeps its task as it was by its own choices (`TaskDemoRecipe.Choose`: its debug tools, a page for every step).
  - **Every choice a setting** (today's values the defaults): `nameSuffixes` (what a tool's name drops as a refusal says it), `labelSeparators` (what splits a buttons row: was English " and " / " or "), `captionPattern` (a picture's caption), `fallbackDistance` (where an arrow or a picture looks from with no viewpoint before it), `names` (new `TaskNames`: the sequence, placard, picture, voices, viewpoints, debug panel and a placed viewpoint's prefix; `TaskKit.SequenceName`, `PlacardName`, `PipName`, `VoiceName`, `ViewpointsName`, `DebugName`, `ViewpointName` take the settings, each with a `ManifestScene` overload). A refusal's reply, a wrong answer's reply, the separators and the caption pattern are read per row. The six types in `TaskSettings.cs` are a file each.
  - **The kit's own writes go through the features**: viewpoints through `ViewpointsActions.AddViewpoint` / `PlannedViewpoint`; a picture's spot through `PanelsActions.AddShotFrom`; the rig through `ViewpointsActions.AddEyes`, `AddTraveler(rig, eyeHeight)`, `GoTo` and `Interactions.EquipUser(..., nearClip)`; arrows' style and removal through `IndicatorsActions.SetStyle` / `RemoveIndicator`; the tuning file's delete through `Placements.DeleteTuning`; the debug panel's removal and the anchors' hiding through new actions, `PanelsActions.RemoveDebugPanel(name, ctx)` and `Placements.HideAnchor(anchor, ctx)`. A task keeps only its own spots' adjustments (`Placements.Capture(path, roots, ctx)` with its delivery and its own top-level objects), so two plans built into one scene keep apart. Grouping and sides are Placement's (`Placer.Cluster`, `Placer.SideDirection`).
  - **Decoupled**: no behavior names another's type (a check finds its list by its partner; `show` is `fade_in`'s builder registered under a second name, `ShowBehavior` removed; a take's signal is `TaskKit.SelectedSignal(node)`, by the node's path where its name repeats); one arrow-naming helper, one row-scaffold for what a viewpoint sees, one "names nothing" check. The behaviors are internal (the kit's tests see them).
  - **Renamed or removed** (no project uses unitytk yet): `TaskKit.EyesName` (`ViewpointsActions.EyesName`), `NearClip` (`Interactions.HeldNearClip`), `SelectBehavior.Signal` (`TaskKit.SelectedSignal`), `ChecklistBehavior.ListName` (`TaskKit.ChecklistName`), `FadeOutBehavior.LeftCode` (`TaskKit.FadeReturnedCode`), `PlayBehavior.FollowsNothingCode` (`TaskKit.FollowsNothingCode`), `OptionBehavior.Correct` (`TaskKit.Correct`); the behavior classes are internal. `TaskKit.AddDebugTools(plan, settings, ctx)` names what it makes by the task's settings. The demo recipe takes a context (`TaskDemoRecipe.Build(folder, ctx)`); its menu runs as one undo step. Docs: `docs/task.md` in the sample's words and step IDs, `docs/parts/kit.md`'s registry corrected (`TypeCache.GetTypesDerivedFrom`), the kit's prior art names Unity's Tutorial Framework.
  - Test(s): red first: `TaskBehaviorsTests` (each role taken by a project's own verb: `TestRolesBehavior`, `TestGateBehavior`), `TaskSettingsTests` (the defaults ship no debug tools and leave a headset's camera and the delivery's meshes alone; a page where a step shows one, or every step; a row's words by the task's settings), `TaskBuildTests` (`Two_plans_built_into_one_scene_each_keep_only_their_own_adjustments`, `A_build_with_its_rig_and_debug_tools_plans_what_it_then_does`), `IndicateBehaviorTests` (one naming; a merge taking the arrows made apart away), `PanelsActionsTests.A_debug_panel_is_taken_away_as_asked_and_undone`, `PlacementsTests.An_anchors_meshes_are_hidden_as_asked_once_and_undone`; `TaskSampleTests` find the demo recipe by its assembly and signature, not a type name.

- **2026-11-24 -- Panels, Placement and Steps: a sequence's autopilot and debug commands are the Steps feature's, a placed spot's move is asked and undone, the tuning file is the package's JSON of a version it reads, and every choice a parameter (`Features/Panels`, `Features/Placement`, `Features/Steps`, `Tools/Placement` new, the Tasks kit's call sites and runtime half).**
  - **Moved and renamed** (GUIDs kept, so a scene's components hold): the Tasks kit's `TaskAutopilot` is the Steps feature's `SequenceAutopilot`, `TaskDebug` its `SequenceDebugCommands` (`RestartCourse` is `RestartSequence`; new `Show()`). Neither names another feature: how the autopilot acts on a step's parts is an `IStepActor` beside it (`Acts`, `Act`, `Release`, `Busy`; `SequenceAutopilot.Actor`), where the commands go an `ICommandSurface` (`Add`, `Remove`, `SetStatus`; `SequenceDebugCommands.Surface`); the kit's runtime half holds the two that compose features (`InteractionActor` over Interaction's targets and clips, its hand moved there from the autopilot; `DebugPanelSurface` over Panels' `DebugPanel`). New actions `StepsActions.AddAutopilot(node, sequence, startEnabled, ctx)`, `AddDebugCommands(node, sequence, ctx)` and their `PlannedObject` overloads; `TaskKit.AddDebugTools` returns `SequenceDebugCommands`.
  - **Placement**: `Placements.Place` asks before it moves a node it adds a spot to (the move was not recorded, so an undo left the node moved) and takes a `PlannedObject` in a dry run (`Place(PlannedObject node, key, ctx)`; `SpotKey(parent, name)` removed: a spot is planned by its node's path, so a dry run and its live run agree on the many nodes of one name a build makes). The tuning file is written by `Core.Json` through `Core.AtomicFile` (the same format; floats as short as they read) and its `version` read: a later one's file is refused, said (`TuningCode`), never overwritten (`TuningVersion`). `Capture(path, roots, ctx)` keeps only the spots under one build's roots (two builds in one scene kept each other's adjustments). `DeleteTuning(path, ctx)` drops a tuning file. `Placer.Cluster` (things closer than a distance as one group), `Placer.SideDirection(StandSide, of, stoodAt)` (`StandSide` new), `Stand(..., bodyRadius, bodyHeight)` (was 0.35 m and its 1.95 m headroom), `FloorNear(..., depth)` (its ray measured from the part, not the world's origin), `DefaultBodyRadius`, `DefaultFloorDepth`. A part's own collider under a rigidbody outside it no longer hides it from itself (`hit.collider`, not `hit.transform`). `Occluders(roots, readableOnly)` passes over a mesh a player cannot cook (`Skipped`). Removed: `Placer.BoundsOf` (`SceneBounds.TryOf`), `Placer`'s own panel cover (`PanelCover.Count`). The Capture Adjustments menu item is the new `Tools/Placement` tool's (its path kept).
  - **Panels**: `Placard` is unsealed, its drawing a `protected virtual bool Render(VisualElement root)`, its `OnEnable` and `LateUpdate` protected virtual. A window added by hand is given the package's panel settings, layout and look (`PanelDefaults`). New fields, today's values the defaults: `pixelsPerMetre` (`Placard.DefaultPixelsPerMetre`; `Placard.PixelsPerMetre` removed), `windowLayer` (`UI`; a picture's camera leaves its own out) and `fold` (`WindowFold`: the arrow's glyphs and tooltips) on the placard, the picture and the debug panel; `maxSwing`, `maxLift` on the picture and the debug panel; `PipView.pictureHeight` (`DefaultPictureHeight`, was 0.27 m), `unscaledTime`; `DebugPanel.releaseBuilds`, `ShowsIn(debugBuild)` (a release build leaves it out where asked). The picture rides the eyes allocating nothing a frame, without a physics sync, and destroys its texture with it. A placard's choice buttons are named by place (`choice-<n>`), Enter styled by a class (`Placard.EnterClass`, was `#key-enter`); `Panels.uss`'s colours are variables on each window's class. New actions `PanelsActions.AddShotFrom(shot, name, ctx)` (a shot's own spot to look from; `FromName`) and its `PlannedObject` overload, `PlannedShot(pip, shot)`. Plan keys of a panel's parts by path; `SceneRoots.Find` for the top-level windows.
  - **Steps**: a choice's outcome is one field, `StepChoice.outcome` (`ChoiceOutcome`: Next, Finish, Restart, Start; `Finishes`, `Restarts`, `Starts`), its `finishes`, `restarts` and `starts` read into it as a saved choice loads. A step awaiting nothing raises `onComplete` as it starts (a page's Next waiting on it never showed), once a run; what a step's completion does to the sequence stands (a listener that finished it threw, one that went elsewhere was skipped past). `StepsActions.SignalOn` wires one event to two signals (the second was taken for wired). `Sequence.reloadOnRestart`, `reloadMode` (Single, or Additive: the scenes loaded beside it kept), `unscaledTime`. Plan keys by path; `SceneRoots.Find<Sequence>`. No "training" or "task" in the features' words.
  - Test(s): red first where a defect: `PlacementsTests` (`Placing_a_fresh_spot_is_one_undo_step_its_move_too`, `A_dry_run_of_a_node_it_only_planned_plans_what_the_live_run_does`, `The_tuning_file_is_the_packages_json_and_a_later_versions_file_is_refused`, `A_capture_scoped_to_one_builds_roots_keeps_only_its_spots`, `Deleting_the_tuning_file_is_asked_and_drops_every_adjustment`), `PlacerTests` (cluster, side, a floor far below the origin, the body's room, a collider under a rigidbody), `OccludersTests`, `SequenceTests` (a step awaiting nothing completes; completion's moves stand; saved switches; restart without reload), `StepsActionsTests` (one event, two signals; debug commands planned, made once, undone), `SequenceAutopilotTests`, `SequenceDebugCommandsTests`, `PlacardTests` (button names, Enter's class, the arrow's words, the render seam), `PipViewTests` (no allocation a frame; its window layer; its texture destroyed), `PanelsActionsTests` (a shot's spot; a window added by hand), `DebugPanelTests` (release builds, drag limits), the kit's `DebugPanelSurfaceTests`.

  - **New on `ManifestBehaviorAttribute`**: `NodeFields`, `TakeFields` (`clip` by default), `FieldLabels` (`name=Label`), `ChipFields` (`{field}` templates), `FieldLabel(field)`, `NamesNode(field)`, `DefaultLabel(field)`; `SheetPlan.FieldValues(action, name)`. An assessment finds only the fields a behavior declares (was `tool`, `facing`, `arrows` and `clip` on every row), and the window labels a row's fields and tells two rows apart by its behavior's declarations (was one kit's labels by name). The Tasks kit declares its own in `TaskBehaviorAttribute` (`tool`, `facing`, `arrows`; Choice, In hand, Says; `'{label}'`, `→ {to}`).
  - **A behavior's kit is one rule** (`ManifestBehaviors.KitOf`, `SettingsOf`): the kit compiled beside its attribute, else beside its builder. The window took the first kit holding a field of the name.
  - **A fresh scene's setups are its kits' own**: `[ManifestSceneSetup(typeof(Kit))]` (`ManifestSceneSetupAttribute.Kit`; none named: the kit compiled beside it, and every manifest's where none is), run by `Reset` only for the kits taking part, each handed the run's context (was null); a setup that fails is said. `Reset` places, builds and records through its context and asks before saving its scene (`ShouldWrite`).
  - **An object's own settings and its row's own fields are kept apart**: `Manifest.Tune.fields` (new; `Tune.Field`, `SetField`, `Empty`), `Manifest.Entry` (the base of `Edit` and `Tune`); `ManifestsActions.SetRowField` (new) sets a row's field, `SetTune` a setting alone, each refusing a name that is not one and saying so (`ManifestsActions.SettingCode`). A setting a sheet's row or an object names that its kit's settings lack is a note on its row (`ManifestsActions.NoteCode`), never dropped (`ManifestScene.Settings<T>(step, target, row)`). `OwnSettings` and `SetTunes` start from what the sheet's row says, so the window for one object shows its row's own settings.
  - **Every write asked, every plan its run's**: the build's record of what it gave its parts is asked of the manifest (an undo takes it back with the build, so an undone part is no longer kept as a person's change); a dry run of a manifest not placed yet plans its rows over an instance in a preview scene, not the placement alone; `Create` raises the plan's own refusal; the kit's defaults made for a run go as it ends (one leaked per assessment).
  - **One reader of a sheet's settings file**: `SheetSettings.g.cs`, generated from `unitytk.sheets.SheetSettings` (`sync_unity_contracts.py`); `Stale` reads it; `SyncCommand`'s operation and arguments are one list, held to the operation's catalog. What a step is built from (`ManifestScene.Source`) names its rows' settings too.
  - **The window**: what a run says that no row says (a refusal, a setup that failed) is shown as its notices until the next run (`ManifestWindow.Reported`): *Build in a New Scene* no longer fails silently; `ManifestWindow.SetRowField`; one Add section for the cell's menu and the **+** chip. **The manifest's inspector** (`ManifestInspector`) edits what a person sets by hand; locks, behaviors off, edits, own values, the build's record and the kits' settings list show read-only, changed through the actions alone.
  - **Removed**: `ManifestScene.TopLevel` (`SceneRoots.Find`; the Tasks kit's calls moved), the Kernel's public `EditorLook` and `WindowMenu`. No "task", "DCC" or "Shot Manifest" in the window's or the statuses' words.
  - Test(s): `ManifestsActionsTests` (what a step is built from; only declared fields looked up; a setting its kit lacks said, settings and fields apart; one object's settings from its row; an assessment leaves no settings behind; a manifest's build from nothing placed planned, undone and converging; a reset asking before it saves, a plan's refusal), `ManifestInspectorTests`, `ManifestBehaviorsTests` (two kits, each with its own behaviors, data and settings, in one manifest; a fresh scene's setups its kits' own), `ManifestWindowTests.A_build_in_a_new_scene_that_cannot_run_says_why_in_the_window`, `ManifestLinesTests` (over a test kit of the tool's own), `WindowMenuTests`; `test_sheet_sync.py` `test_the_unity_manifests_command_names_the_operation_and_its_arguments`.

- **2026-11-24 -- Interaction, Indicators and Viewpoints: a timed press plays on as it is let go, a cue switched off as its holdable is taken is the feature's own wiring, every choice a parameter, every plan key the kernel's (`Features/Interaction` with its uGUI and XRI doors, `Features/Indicators`, `Features/Viewpoints`, `Kits/Tasks` call sites).**
  - **Fixed**: `ClipTrigger.Release` paused a timed press's clip once it had played (only a trigger played while held now stops or rewinds as it is let go). A refused gesture said so twice (`PointerSelect`'s press and click, `XRSelect`'s select and press: one now, and the XR door finds the hand once). `SelectNear` checked the first hand in the scene each frame (now the hand holding what the target requires, `Holdable.Holder`). Both input doors gave a target's colliders, planning the removal of a skinned part's bound collider twice (`MakeTarget` gives them once, before the inputs wire). `Target.Enabled` is emptied as play starts without a domain reload. The XRI door's runtime assembly is defined by `UNITYTK_XRI`, as its editor half (was `UNITYTK_TOOLKIT`).
  - **New actions**: `Interactions.DisableOnTake(GameObject, Behaviour, ctx)` and its `PlannedObject` overload (a part switched off as its holdable is taken: the Tasks kit's arrows no longer wire `onTaken` themselves), `Interactions.IsHoldable`, `Interactions.EquipUser(eyes, handAt, float nearClip, ctx)` (`HeldNearClip`), `Interactions.AddClip(root, clip, WindowRules, ctx)`; `IndicatorsActions.AddMarker(PlannedObject, ctx)`, `SetStyle` (an indicator's colour and size, also one only planned), `RemoveIndicator`; `ViewpointsActions.AddEyes` (`EyesName`), `AddTraveler(rig, float eyeHeight, ctx)`, `GoTo(traveler, viewpoint, back, ctx)`, `AddViewpoint(holder, name, scene, ctx)` with `PlannedViewpoint`, `AddTravelTo(PlannedObject, ctx)`, `AddTravelTo(PlannedObject, facing, ctx)`, `AddFacing(PlannedObject, facing, ctx)`, `FacingName(PlannedObject, facing)`.
  - **Changed**: `bool startEnabled = false` before `ctx` on `AddSendHome`, `AddClipTrigger`, `AddSelectNear`, `AddMarker`, `AddIndicator` (each overload) and `AddTravelTo`; `AddMarker(target, bool ownMesh = false, ...)` and `Marker.ownMesh` false by default (a marker no longer hides the mesh of what it marks unless asked; the Tasks kit asks); `AddIndicator(target, name, bool follow = false, ...)` (was decided by the child's name); `AddTravelTo(viewpoint, facing, float standOff = 0, bool startEnabled = false, ctx)` (`ctx` last; was `(viewpoint, facing, ctx, standOff)`); `AddColliders(node, bool convex = false, ctx)`; `PointerActions.SetUpPointer(camera, bool assist = true, ctx)`; `Interactions.Window(clip, tools, WindowRules)` and `PosedAfter(root, clips, WindowRules)` (`Interactions.WindowRules`: `still`, `popSeconds`, `popTimes`); `TravelTo.Travel()` returns nothing (a UnityEvent calls it), `TryTravel()` says whether it brought the user. Callers passing `ctx` by position pass it by name.
  - **Fields** (today's values the defaults): `Holdable.paceDistance`, `paceLimits`; `PointerSelect.button`; `Marker.width`, `sides`, `lift`, `color`; `Indicator.outlineColor`; `Traveler.feet` (a rig's floor, where its origin is not); `TravelTo.floorRule` (`TravelFloor`: its mesh's top, its pivot, the first surface below it, a height), `floorHeight`, `rayFrom`, `TravelTo.FloorUnder`, `Stand(..., floorRule, floorHeight, rayFrom)`; `unscaledTime` on `ClipTrigger`, `ClipPlayer`, `Holdable`, `Marker`, `Indicator`.
  - **One home**: plan keys through `OpContextEdits.Key` / `Label` (`IndicatorsActions`, `ViewpointsActions`, `PointerInput`, `XRInput`, `PointerActions`, `Interactions`' own `Label` removed; a change to one of two same-named nodes says its path, so a dry run plans both); the Tasks kit's restated keys (`TeleportBehavior.Planned`, `MarkerBehavior`, `TaskKit.AddDebugTools`, `TaskKit.IsHoldable`) call the features' planned overloads; bounds, eyes and materials through `SceneBounds`, `Viewer` and `MaterialWriter.Plain` (`Marker`, `Indicator`, `TravelTo`, `PointerActions`). Docs, tooltips and plan text in neutral words; no production figures or producer checks in comments.
  - Test(s): red first: `ClipTriggerTests.A_timed_press_held_long_enough_plays_its_clip_on_after_it_is_let_go`, `A_click_plays_its_clip_on_whatever_a_release_says`, `PointerSelectTests.A_click_refused_says_so_once`, `XRSelectTests.A_controllers_select_refused_says_so_once`, `HandTests.A_holdable_added_by_hand_rests_where_it_stood_as_the_scene_starts`, `ClipPlayerTests.A_clip_played_again_takes_the_place_of_its_finished_layer_and_the_poses_stand`, `IndicatorTests.A_marker_rings_its_targets_foot_leaving_its_mesh_alone_unless_asked_to_show_it`, `InteractionsTests.A_skinned_part_two_inputs_wire_plans_its_bound_colliders_removal_once_as_it_is_removed_once`, and two objects of one name planned apart (`IndicatorsActionsTests`, `ViewpointsActionsTests`, `PointerSelectTests`, `XRSelectTests`); new: `PointerLookTests`, the timed press, `SetPress`, `PlayAfter`, `FitTool`, `Hand.InScene`, `DisableOnTake` (live, planned, undone), the window's rules, the near clip, the pace, the button, the assist, the marker's and outline's colours, `SetStyle`, `RemoveIndicator`, `AddEyes`, `AddTraveler(eyeHeight)`, `GoTo`, `AddViewpoint` with its planned chain, the floor rules, a rig's feet, `Travel` from a UnityEvent; the window test's takes named neutrally.
- **2026-11-24 -- The sheet plan names no kit's data, and the sheet reader's rules are data (`unitytk.sheets`, `Features/Manifests`, `Tools/Manifests`, `Kits/Tasks`; unitytk 0.9.0, needing pythontk 0.26.0, which releases first).**
  - **Plan contract version 2** (`SheetPlan.VERSION`, `SheetPlan.g.cs` regenerated; a version-1 plan is refused: read its sheet again). A row's words beyond its behavior, nodes, take and jump are its `fields`, each the group of its clause template (was `tool`, `facing`, `side`, `side_of`, `arrows`, `label`, `value`, `phrase`); a step's values are its `fields`, named by the reading (was `voice`, `placard`, `hint`, and `audio_file`, now `sound`); a kit's plan-wide data is its entry of `extensions`, as the settings file gives it (was `tools` and `PlanTool`: the Tasks kit's `extensions.task.tools` now); `PlanSource.clauses_digest`. C#: `SheetPlan.Field`, `FieldList`, `SetField`, `DeclaredFields`, `Extension`; `ManifestScene.Asset<T>(path)`, `AssetFolder` and `FindAsset` (were `Sound`, `SoundFolder`, `Sounds`); `Manifest.assetFolder` (was `soundFolder`, kept by `FormerlySerializedAs`); `ManifestsActions.AssetFolder` (was `SoundFolder`) and `IsRowField` (was `RowFields`: a behavior's own fields come from its attribute's `Fields`); a step's source line names its jump and every field. Python: `PlanAction.field`, `PlanStep.field`, `SheetPlan.with_extensions` (was `with_tools`), `SheetSettings.extensions` (was `tools`), the `sheet.sync` result's `missing_files` (was `missing_sounds`). Tasks: `TaskKit.Extension`, `ToolsCode` (a tool no row puts in hand, a fit that is no [x, y, z]: raised as the kit builds).
  - **The reading is data** (`ReadingSpec`, `sheets/readings/*.json`, `default.json` under each template's): strictness, the name rules (`inline`, `suffixes`, `viewpoint`, `arrow`), a step ID's shape (a clause template's `{step}`; was a regex in three templates and in the converter), the step fields and the files staging copies, the Action cell's words (templates by name), a question's mark, the phrase matcher's vocabulary (`NodeMatcher(vocabulary=)`: synonyms, stop and distinct words, qualifiers, opposites) and a format's columns. pythontk's mapping `strict` and `names` and its behavior templates' `clause` form move here (`sheets/clauses/highlight.json`, `fade_in.json`, `fade_out.json`; `ClauseBook.shared_templates` removed). `SheetPlan.from_sheet(reading=)`, `take_list(reading=)`, `SheetCheck.run(reading=)` (its `names` folded into the reading), `SheetSettings.reading()`, `book()`.
  - **Roles, not names**: every built-in clause template declares its `roles` and the groups that name a node (`ClauseSpec.nodes`); new roles `points`, `places`, `destination`, `continues`, `on_page`, `page`, `entry`, `entered`, `otherwise`, `questions`, `lists`, `ticks`; a role declared with settings belongs to the rows carrying them (`ClauseBook.roles(behavior, settings)`, `templates_with`, `nodes`). The reading pass and the check name no behavior. A `note` template (`Note: ...`).
  - **A project's clause templates live beside its settings file** (`SheetSettings.clauses`, `clauses/` by default; `ClauseBook(folder=, step=)`, `ClauseBook.digest`): no per-user folder, so a sheet reads the same on every machine.
  - **Build suffixes and renamed nodes reach every path**: the tolerant reading's matcher is built after the reading's suffixes; a row's own names keep the delivery's suffixes and renamed nodes (`DeliveryIndex.subset`); `with_suffixes` keeps the keyed behaviors; `merged` keeps both files' keyed pairs of one step; what a step shows is matched by the delivery's own suffixes. A carrier is any node `SceneStoreBase.is_carrier` names (`NS:data_export`, `data_export1`, `data_export.001`), read with its path, the scene's own first, its record through `SceneRecords.SHOTS.decode`; an FBX is read with its media skipped.
  - **One default template** (`DEFAULT_TEMPLATE`: Interactive) for the plan, the take list, the check and the settings. The check parses the sheet once, names each column by the template's own header and reads blanks from its `exclude_values`, in neutral words; a hint line's cell is `(Hint): ...`; the take list's text column is headed as the template heads it. The converter's target is data (`SheetConverter.convert(to=, options=)`: the target reading's columns, a step ID spelled as the target's step pattern reads it back; its old sheet's options reach the reading; `COLUMNS` removed). `sheet.sync` compares and copies the delivery's files in chunks. Typed `ctx` arguments; the sheets package ruff-formatted; `operations.json` rewritten.
  - Test(s): red first where a defect: `test_sheet_plan.py` `LampFixtureTestCase` (a second fixture: build suffixes on the tolerant and the strict paths, what a step shows, a step ID's shape, the vocabulary, roles alone), `ContractTestCase`, `ProjectClausesTestCase`, `DeliveryIndexTestCase` (carriers, groupings keep every reading, merged keyed pairs), `ClauseBookTestCase.test_the_roles_are_declared_by_the_templates`; `test_sheet_settings.py` (project templates and the digest, validated name rules, extensions); `test_sheet_converter.py` `test_the_target_format_and_the_old_sheets_options_are_data`; `test_sheet_check.py` `SheetCheckReadsOnceTestCase`; Edit Mode `SheetPlanTests.A_rows_fields_and_a_kits_extension_are_read_by_name`, the Manifests, Tools/Manifests and Tasks suites on the new shape (75 passed); the Tasks sample's plan regenerated.
- **2026-11-24 -- One home for the scene helpers several parts wrote each their own; one plan key, and a planned object an action takes in a dry run (`Kernel`, `Features/Lightmaps`, `ShadowPlane`, `RenderEffects`, `Looks`, `AnimationAudio`, `Shots`, `ArticulatedRig`).**
  - **The plan key** (`OpContextEdits.Key(GameObject, Type)`, `Label`: the name, else the path where the name repeats in its scene, as `Interactions` did alone): every action in these features asks by it, so a dry run and its live run agree on two objects of one name. **`PlannedObject`**: what an action is handed for an object a dry run only planned (`OpContextEdits.Key(PlannedObject, Type)`, `PlanCreate`), so a caller never restates an action's key.
  - **Scene helpers** (`Kernel/Runtime`): `SceneBounds` (the renderer-bounds union, switched-off renderers, inactive objects, a skipped branch and a fallback size as parameters, an allocation-free form over a kept list, and the meshes' box in a space, by corners or every vertex), `Viewer` (the eyes given, else the main camera), `PanelCover` (what a panel before the eyes hides), `MaterialWriter.Default`, `Plain`, `Paint` (a part's own material, the pipeline's default copied, coloured and glowing); `SceneRoots` (`Kernel/Editor`: a scene's top-level object by name). The Lightmaps importer's model box, ShadowPlane's tight box, `SceneCapture`'s framing, `AddSoundCue`'s holder and `ArticulatedRigPointerGrab`'s camera use them.
  - Test(s): `SceneBoundsTests`, `ViewerTests`, `PanelCoverTests`, `SceneRootsTests`, `PlannedObjectTests`, `OpContextTests` (the key by path where a name repeats; a planned part planned once), `MaterialWriterTests` (a plain material), `RenderEffectsActionsTests` (a repeated node named by its path).
- **2026-11-24 -- The baked surface's shader is the Lightmaps feature's, drawing the hand-off's settings the set holds; `IRenderPipelineHost.SetBaked` takes the shader (`Kernel`, `Kernel/URP`, `Features/Lightmaps`).**
  - **Moved**: `Kernel/URP/BakedLit.shader` and `.hlsl` to the Lightmaps feature's URP door (`Features/Lightmaps/URP/`, GUIDs kept; the shader's name stays `unitytk/Baked Lit`). **Changed**: `IRenderPipelineHost.SetBaked(Material)` is `SetBaked(Material, Shader baked)`: a part brings its shader written over the pipeline's Lit, and the host moves a material on its Lit onto it where it draws it (`RenderPipelineHosts.SwapLit`; Built-in's host now does too). **Removed**: `UniversalRenderPipelineHost.BakedLitShader` (now `LightmapsImporter.BakedLitShader`).
  - **Its settings are the record's, not one viewer's policy**: the binding set publishes its `reflectionScale` (`lightmappedMaterials.envMapIntensity`) and `reflectionByBake` (`reflectionNormalization: lightmapLuma`) as `LightmapSet.BakedShader`, and the light of the environment its surfaces reflect (its probe's room, else the scene's ambient light) as `ProbeShader`; both go to zero once no set binds, and the shader then draws as Lit (`_UnitytkProbeSH` was never cleared). Globals, not material properties: one outside Lit's own buffer would take every baked material out of the SRP Batcher.
  - Test(s): `BakedLitTests` (its forward pass is the installed Lit's; a Lit material takes it with its values), `UniversalRenderPipelineHostTests` and `RenderPipelineHostsTests` (the swap, and the Built-in host's `SetBaked`, `DisplaySize` and `Display`), `LightmapSetTests` (what it publishes, and none once no set binds).
- **2026-11-24 -- A Lightmap Set holds the scene only as its hand-off says, only over the lights that light its renderers alone, and never into a saved scene (`Features/Lightmaps`, `Contracts`).**
  - **Both rules off by default** (`LightmapSet.lightsOff`, `environment`): a set added by hand leaves the scene's lights and environment alone; the import sets each from the file's `handoff` record (`keyLight.disabledWhen` lightmapped, `environment.source` the probe), with `environmentIntensity` (`probeIntensity`) and the reflection settings beside them. A value the set does not draw (an `envMapTerms` other than specular) is said in the import log. **Lights Off** now switches off only a realtime light that lights the mapped renderers and nothing else (its Culling Mask and, short of a directional light, its range), never every light reaching them.
  - **The handoff record's reader is a contract** (`Contracts/HandoffBlock.cs` with `HandoffBlock.g.cs`, `HandoffRendering.g.cs`, moved from `Features/Looks/Editor`; `HandoffBlock.Read`, `States`, `Text`, `Number`): the Looks and Lightmaps importers both read it.
  - **A save keeps none of it**: around every save of a scene each bound set gives back the lights it switched off, the environment and its renderers' fields, and binds again once saved (`LightmapOwner`'s `sceneSaving` / `sceneSaved` hook; `LightmapSet.Suspend`, `Resume`). Measured first: without it the saved scene kept the light off and the set's ambient mode.
  - Test(s): `LightmapSetTests` (a set added by hand; only the lights that light its renderers alone; the saved scene, with the hook taken away and with it), `LightmapsImporterTests` (the handoff's rules and settings onto the set; the records it reads).
- **2026-11-24 -- A look's table, span, cameras and weight are its own, and a save keeps none of it (`Features/Looks`, `Kernel`, `Kernel/URP`).**
  - `SceneLook.table` (a lookup table of its own in place of a named curve), `span` (the brightest light it spans), `cameras` (empty: every game camera), `priority`, `bloomOff`, `vignetteOff`; `LookCurve.Table(..., span)`. The host's display takes them: `IRenderPipelineHost.Display(display, scale, out why, DisplayOptions options = null)` (URP: the volume's priority, bloom and vignette off as asked, post-processing on for the cameras named). Two looks at one priority no longer claim the second shows over the first.
  - A save puts every shown look away first (what it changed on a camera given back) and shows it again once saved (`SceneLookSaving`). A look is never shown in a scene the editor only previews in (a model's import, Prefab Mode: `SceneLook.Previewed`), and a pipeline that reads no lookup table (Built-in) is said once at import, not by each placed copy.
  - Test(s): `SceneLookTests` (its own table, span, weight and cameras; a save puts it away and shows it again; none in a preview scene; a placed copy quiet in Built-in), `LookCurveTests` (a table's span).
- **2026-11-24 -- Fix Texture Roles respects a project's own texture cap (`Features/Materials`).** Only a texture still at Unity's default cap (2048) is raised to its own size, on its first import or by `MaterialsActions.FixTextureRoles`; a cap a project or a preset chose stays. **Changed**: `FixTextureRoles(Material, OpContext)` is `FixTextureRoles(Material, bool fullSize = true, OpContext ctx = null)` (false: every cap stays).
  - Test(s): `MaterialsActionsTests` (Unity's cap raised, a project's kept, none touched where asked).
- **2026-11-24 -- What an effect or a sound cue does is its caller's to say (`Features/RenderEffects`, `Features/AnimationAudio`).**
  - `RenderEffectsActions.AddHighlight(node, pulse, bool startEnabled = false, ctx)`, `AddFadeTo(node, alpha, seconds = FadeTo.DefaultSeconds, bool startEnabled = false, ctx)`, `AnimationAudioActions.AddSoundCue(owner, cue, sound, bool startEnabled = false, float spatialBlend = 0, bool loop = false, ctx)`: a part made switched on where asked; callers passing `ctx` by position pass it by name.
  - `FadeTo.DefaultSeconds`, the effect recipe's fade (`EffectRecipeDefaults.FadeFrames` at its reference rate), replaces the 0.5 s literal; `FadeTo.unscaledTime` and `Highlight.unscaledTime` run on real time. A pulse whose duty and ramp outrun its swing falls to dim before the next rise instead of jumping.
  - `SoundCue.spatialBlend`, `loop` and `setSource` (off: it plays its AudioSource as that is set up, its own clip, looping and blend).
  - Test(s): `RenderEffectsActionsTests` (switched on where asked; the recipe's length), `HighlightTests` (no jump at the wrap), `AnimationAudioActionsTests` (a cue placed, looping, or playing its source).
- **2026-11-24 -- The project builder tunes the owned project's rendering alone unless asked; a player compile names no editor assembly (`unitytk.project_builder`, `unitytk.unity_compile`).**
  - `ProjectBuilder.build(tuned=None)`: tunes the owned project's URP rendering (MSAA, Forward, screen-space occlusion off) and leaves a keyed project's as its template made it; `tuned=True` or `False` says otherwise. `tune_pipeline(..., pipeline_settings, renderer_settings, features_off)`, today's values the defaults.
  - `UnityCompile`: a player build referenced a project's `UnityEditor.*` assemblies (uGUI's `UnityEditor.UI`, whose name holds no `.Editor`); now left out of it.
  - Test(s): `test_project_builder.py` (the owned project tuned, a keyed one only when asked; the tuning's parameters), `test_unity_compile.py` (uGUI a package, the .NET Framework shims named by the editor build alone, no editor assembly in the player's).
- **2026-11-24 -- Small fixes in the foundation and its features (`Kernel`, `Features/Lightmaps`).** `ProbeCube.Turned`'s summary was on `Ambient`; the stand-in host's display disposal (`PureRenderPipelineHost`) no longer names a nested `Undo` that hid `UnityEditor.Undo`; the foundation guide lists the new helpers.
- **2026-11-23 -- Each object's own settings over the task's, kept from the scene too; one behavior read several ways, its chip saying which; a skinned part found by its bones (`Features/Manifests`, `Tools/Manifests`, `Interaction`, `Steps`, `RenderEffects`, `Kits/Tasks`, `unitytk.sheets`).**
  - **An object's own values** (`Manifest.tunes`, `ManifestsActions.SetTune`, `SetTunes`, `OwnSettings`; `ManifestScene.Settings<T>(step, target, row)`: the task's, then what the sheet's row says, then the object's own): any setting of a behavior for one object (a button held to play, an arrow's colour), and the fields of a row (the take it plays, the step it goes to), each kept as an edit, marked amber, reverted with the object's edits. The chip's **Settings for 'X'…** edits them; **Settings for Every Row…** the task's. Every behavior can be added to an object (`Assignable` no longer drops one that needs a take or a tool): what it lacks, its settings give (`ManifestBehaviorAttribute.Fields`).
  - **What a person changes in the scene on a part a behavior built is kept** (`IManifestBound`; `Manifest.built`): at the next build it becomes the object's own value (an arrow resized or recoloured stays so), while a change of the task's settings still reaches every part no one changed. Arrows: `TaskSettings.arrowColor` (the highlight's), size and colour bound.
  - **One row of a step told apart from another of its behavior** (`Manifest.Edit.row`, `Manifest.Tune.row`: its place among the step's rows; -1 every row): a marker shown then hidden in one step, a step's two gotos, edited apart.
  - **One behavior, read several ways; its chip says which** (`ClauseSpec.settings`, `PlanAction.settings`, regenerated; `ManifestBehaviorAttribute.Suffix`): `next` is now `pause` (`TaskSettings.pauseWhen`: as the step finishes, or at its start, the button starting its actions: `StepChoice.starts`, `StepsActions.SetStarts`, `Step.Started`); `press_hold` reads as `use` held to play; `marker_show` / `marker_hide` are `marker` (`markerShow`); `pip_hide` is `pip` (`pipShow`); `answer_else` is `answer` (`answerKind`). Chips: *Pause · start*, *Marker · hide*, *Use · hold to play*. The sheet's words are read as before; the Action word *press and hold* reads as its reading. Retired: `NextBehavior`, `PressHoldBehavior`, `MarkerShowBehavior`, `MarkerHideBehavior`, `PipHideBehavior`, `AnswerElseBehavior`.
  - **A Use row plays its take on a click unless set otherwise** (`TaskSettings.useTrigger`: Click, HoldToPlay, TimedPress; `pressSeconds`; `ClipTrigger.pressSeconds`, `PressProgress`; `Interactions.SetPress`): per object, never by default. Replaces the round's `useWhileHeld`.
  - **A highlight pulses as long as its step runs, or once** (`Highlight.loop`, `RenderEffectsActions.SetLoop`, `TaskSettings.highlightLoop`).
  - **A skinned part is found by a box on each bone** (`Interactions.BoneBoxes`, `LeastBoneBox`; `AddColliders` removes a collider of the mesh as bound): fitted once to what each bone carries, moving with it, no bake or cook at run time. Measured: the per-frame skin bake left the lower plug's collider empty after a take, without a word from Unity. Retired (never released): `SkinnedCollider`.
  - **The sheet's fault is the manifest's to say, not a click's to hide**: the click forwarding (`Target.Resolve`) is gone; a place whose slot is part of what it places names the sheet's fault and both repairs (the sheet's Asset Names, or Place moved to the right node with its Take and Tool set here).
  - Test(s): `ManifestsActionsTests` (an object's own values, split rows, added rows, revert; a scene change kept, a task change reaching the rest; two rows told apart); `ManifestLinesTests` (a behavior read several ways says which); `SequenceTests` (a choice that starts its step); `TaskKitTests` (a click unless an object of its own is held or timed; the place's repairs); `InteractionsTests` (a box on each bone); `test_sheet_plan.py` (each reading's behavior and settings; the Action word's reading).
- **2026-11-23 -- A click reaches the target that can take it; a picture stays put as its window moves; arrows out of their mount, never snapping; hold to play; a step goes on by itself by default; Back restores the scene; a chip says what tells its row apart (`Interaction`, `Placement`, `Indicators`, `Panels`, `Steps`, `Tools/Manifests`, `Kits/Tasks`).**
  - **A selection a target cannot take goes to the target around it that can** (`Target.Resolve`; `PointerSelect` and `XRSelect` send a press and its release to the same one): a click on a slot inside the part a step first takes takes the part. Measured on a production task: clicks on the new part reached the slot inside it, which refused an empty hand.
  - **A place whose slot is part of what it places is unresolved** (`PlaceBehavior.Check`): the slot would ride into the hand, so the row asks for where the part goes.
  - **A skinned part's collider takes its pose until it holds** (`SkinnedCollider`: baked again each frame while its collider refuses the pose; said only after 120 frames of refusals): measured, a collider refused the pose at start and the lower plug took no clicks.
  - **Moving a picture's window never moves its picture** (`PipView.Place` aims its camera, a child of the card, on the part again).

  - **A flat arrow seen nearly end-on blends to face the eyes** (`Indicator.Face`, from 10 to 20 degrees off its axis): it snapped at one angle (some 75 degrees), flickering as a head moved about it.

  - **An object its step returns goes back shown** (`TaskSettings.fadeReturned`, off; the row notes it, `FadeOutBehavior.LeftCode`): "Keys fade away from hand" with "Return keys to table" left the keys hidden on the table.

  - **Back restores the scene** (`TaskDebug.StepBack`: the scene loaded again, the autopilot walking to the step before at once; `Sequence.Restart` loads it again in the editor's Play Mode too, through `SequenceReload` in the Steps editor part; `Sequence.Reloads`; the autopilot stops at its `stopAt`): Back went to the step before with the scene as the later step left it.
  - **A chip says what tells its row apart, and every field the sheet gives it** (`ManifestLines.Token.detail`, `fields`; its menu's *From the Sheet*): a step's two gotos showed as two bare *Goto*. A step's own chips have a tooltip before an assessment too, and a step's own added behavior no longer shows as an object line with no name.
  - **A dry run plans both changes where re-placing a spot or turning a trigger makes two** (`Placements.Place`, `Interactions.AddClipTrigger`): `&&` skipped the second in a dry run.

- **2026-11-23 -- Behaviors as chips, the same in both manifests; what the delivery made, object by object; a step's own behaviors edited as an object's (`Tools/Manifests`, `Features/Manifests`, `unitytk.sheets`).**

- **2026-11-23 -- The manifest: a scene a build sheet sets up, from one generic part (`Features/Manifests` new, `Tools/Manifests` new, `unitytk.sheets` renamed from `unitytk.sheets`, `sheet.sync` new).** What built a task from a sheet was the Tasks kit's own loop, window and asset, named for trainings; a sheet-built scene is not a task's alone. The loop is now the `Manifests` feature, a behavior's builder is found by name whatever kit registers it, and the Tasks kit is the first set of behaviors. Docs: [the manifest](docs/manifest.md), [the Tasks kit](docs/task.md).
  - **Moved and renamed (no project uses unitytk: no forwarders).** C#: `SheetPlan` -> `Manifests.SheetPlan`; `TaskScene` -> `ManifestScene`; `TaskAssessment` -> `ManifestAssessment`; `ITaskBehavior` -> `IManifestBehavior`; `TaskBehaviors.Find` / `Names` -> `ManifestBehaviors`; `TaskCourse` -> `Manifest` (Create > unitytk > Manifest; its script GUID kept); `TaskCourses.Assess` / `Rebuild` / `Reset` and `TaskKit.Assess` / `Build` -> `ManifestsActions.Assess` / `Build` / `Reset`; `[TaskSceneSetup]` -> `[ManifestSceneSetup]`; `TaskManifestWindow` -> `Tools.Manifests.ManifestWindow` (Tools > unitytk > Manifest); issue codes `unity.task.unsupported` / `unresolved` / `task` -> `unity.manifest.*`. Python: `unitytk.sheets` -> `unitytk.sheets`, `SheetPlan` -> `SheetPlan`, the clause template set `training_clauses` -> `sheet_clauses`.
  - **The kit plugs in** (`IManifestKit`, `TaskBuild`): what a kit builds around its rows (`Begin`, `End`), keeps of a person's adjustments (`Keep`, `Forget`) and its settings type. A kit takes part in a build of every plan holding one of its rows (a behavior its assembly builds, or one marked with its attribute), and of no other. `[TaskBehavior]` derives from `[ManifestBehavior]` and keeps its traits (`TaskBehaviors.Traits`). A kit's state for a build is the scene's (`ManifestScene.State<T>`, `Settings<T>`, `Use`): `TaskState` (the sequence's name, the tuning), and `TaskScene` is now the kit's helpers over a `ManifestScene` (`Sequence`, `Tuning`, `TuningPath`, `EnableDuring`...). `TaskSettings` is an asset a manifest keeps (`Manifest.settings`, `ManifestsActions.AddSettings`), with the tuning file and the debug-tools switch that were the task's.
  - **An assessment is the build's own dry run, row by row.** `ManifestsActions.Assess` runs the build dry and hands each row what the build would change for it (`Row.changes`) and what it holds in the scene (`Row.parts`: a builder says so through `ManifestScene.Hold`; a kit says a step's own part inside `ManifestScene.For`), each step its own (`ManifestAssessment.Step`), and what the steps share (`shared`). A row with nothing to change is built, so what the table says and what Build then does cannot differ. `ManifestStatus` is the one vocabulary (`valid`, `missing`, `update`, `locked`, `missing_object`, `ambiguous_object`, `missing_take`, `sheet`, `unbuildable`, `unknown_behavior`, `warned`: severity, a few words, a sentence), sharing the Shot Manifest's words where they mean the same; `ManifestAssessment.Plan()` is the Build button's count and sentence. A dry run after a person moved a spot plans by what would be kept (`IManifestKit.Keep(manifest, scene, ctx)`), so an adjusted spot never reads as to rebuild. Before the delivery is placed, an assessment says what the plan alone can.
  - **Locks and scoped builds.** `Manifest.locked`, `ManifestsActions.SetLocked`: a build leaves a locked step as it is. `Build(..., only: steps)` builds the named steps (what the steps share is built with them). A build reports progress a step at a time, each a cancel checkpoint.
  - **The Manifest window is the launchpad** (`ManifestWindow`, `ManifestLines`), with the Shot Manifest's layout and words: the source at the top with its notices, Setup (the manifest's fields and each kit's settings, edited in place), Select / Expand / Collapse / Hide / Show, a table of steps and rows (an icon for the state's shape, a few words in Status, every issue in the tooltip, nothing tinted), a detail of the selected line (the sheet's words, each issue with Copy where its fix is upstream, each part with Select and Edit, what a build would change; a row that only sets its step up offers its step's own parts), and the summary beside Export, Assess and Build (N). A row's menu leads with its own fix. It assesses again by itself as the scene changes while an assessment is quick (`AutoAssessBudget`), and selecting an object in the scene selects the row that holds it. New... makes a manifest from a staged plan (`ManifestsActions.Create`).
  - **One action from the sheet to the project** (`unitytk.sheets.SheetSync`, the operation `sheet.sync`): the plan, the delivery's files and each step's sound staged under the project's `Assets`, only what differs written; a dry run plans. The plan now says what it was read from (`PlanSource.sheet_digest`, `settings_digest`, `settings`: the digests fold CRLF to LF, so a checkout's line endings are no change), so the window says when the sheet, its settings or a delivery file changed since (`ManifestsActions.Stale`) and offers the command (`SyncCommand`). `SheetPlan.text()`; `SheetSettings.extra_paths()`, `path`.
  - From an independent review the same day (no wrong-result finding; four defects, fixed): `Reset` is refused while a step is locked (it built a new scene without the locked steps and saved it over the manifest's); a build or a reset keeps a person's adjustments only where the manifest's delivery is placed in the open scene (it captured another plan's spots otherwise); `Stale` reads a sheet another program has open, and a file it cannot read is "cannot tell", never an exception; the window holds its manifest over a new scene (a serialized field, and read again by its GUID). Also: an assessment walks a locked step too, so the step still lists its parts; a scoped build marks only the steps it built (`Step.made`); `sheet.sync` refuses two delivery files of one name and a sound outside the settings file's folder before writing anything; `AddSettings` saves the manifest it changed; discarding a tuning file deletes it as an asset, with its `.meta`; Setup's inspectors are made once, so a field being edited keeps its focus; the window reads the plan and what it was read from once an assessment, not on every redraw. The Tasks sample's demo recipe builds through the task's manifest (made beside its plan once the new scene is open).
  - `Placements.Capture` writes no file where there is none and no spot was adjusted (an empty tuning file is no record): a first build leaves none behind, and the first assessment after it has nothing to do.
  - Test(s): Edit Mode 601 passed, the full suite, in a tooling project of its own (`ManifestsActionsTests` 8, `ManifestWindowTests` 4, `ManifestLinesTests` 2, `SheetPlanTests` and `ManifestSceneTests` moved; `TaskBuildTests` 4, one new: a built task says what each row, step and the task holds, and a moved arrow is kept, never to rebuild); Python `test_sheet_sync.py` new (6), the reader's modules 117 passed, the full Python suite 338. Read on a private 61-step task: 212 rows, an assessment in 1.8 s, the scene left unchanged; the window captured in a windowed editor.

  - The arrow an `Indicator` makes is flat by default (`flat`, `eyes`, `Eyes`): a 13 cm arrow with a thin dark outline that keeps pointing at its target while it turns about its own axis to face the user's eyes wherever they stand (facing them whole when they look straight down it); `flat` off makes the solid one below. The placement search leaves room for it to turn. Before that, the arrow an `Indicator` makes became an arrow (a head and a shaft, 11 cm) glowing in its `color` (`glow` times it, so a filmic tone map keeps it bright; `Indicator.color`, `glow`, `DefaultColor`), casting no shadow; the kit's take the highlight's colour (`Highlight.DefaultColor`, now public).
  - A tool's way to its work and back keeps to free space and its pace: it curves out by the line the user sees the target along, is lined up and turned short of the target, goes straight in along its tip's axis, comes out the same way, and takes longer for a long way (`Holdable.eyes`, `TravelFor`); `travelSeconds` 0 puts it there at once. A task's `tools` take `seconds` (the plan's `PlanTool.seconds`; `Interactions.FitTool(tool, tip, turn, seconds, ctx)`).
  - A task's manifest in Unity, as the Shot Manifest is in the DCC (Tools > unitytk > Tasks Manifest, `TaskManifestWindow`): a `TaskCourse` asset (Create > unitytk > Tasks Task: its plan, delivery, extras, scene, tuning file, sound folder, debug tools) shown step by step, each step's rows with what they act on, their status (ready, unresolved, unsupported, warned) and why, filtered by words or issues, a row selecting what it acts on; Assess checks it against the open scene, Rebuild builds it there keeping what a person adjusted (the moved spots captured first), Reset builds it anew in a new scene, keeping or discarding the adjustments as asked; a plan older than its sheet is flagged. Its actions are `TaskCourses` (`Plan`, `TuningPath`, `SoundFolder`, `PlanStale`, `Placed`, `SceneOf`, `Assess`, `Rebuild`, `Reset`); a project's own setup of a fresh scene (a rig, its delivery's look) is a `[TaskSceneSetup]` method.
  - Found in a full play-through: a spot a teleport brings the user to now also sees what they act on there, until the next teleport (`Placer.Stand(..., also)`; the plugs, latch and solenoid on the open door's inner face were out of sight from the front); an arrow at a part the user takes is switched off once it is taken (its holdable's `onTaken`), not carried into the hand; the debug panel rides lower, its status line in view. An arrow is placed across the line of sight, never pointing at the eyes (seen end-on, a flat arrow showed a sliver at the screws), and one seen nearly end-on faces the eyes whole.
  - From a rules review: `TaskCourse.debugTools` off now takes a task's debug tools away on a rebuild (a switch does its whole job); `TaskDebug` no longer builds its status line every frame; a task's tool `seconds` that is no number is an issue, not a crash; one home for the defaults the kit read twice (`PipView.DefaultFieldOfView`, `DefaultMargin`, `Placard.DefaultHalfExtents`).
  - Play-test 7: a target that is several things apart ("Arrows point at both door locks") gets an arrow at each, not one between them (`Placer.Items`, `Placer.MostItems`); `ClipPlayer`'s private `Start(clip, worker, at)` is `Begin`, ending Unity's "Start() can not take parameters" script error on every reload.
  - Generic and flexible, from a review: a task's own choices are its own (`TaskSettings`: stand-off, PiP style, anchor name patterns, reading pace, least dwell, voice tail, a wrong answer's reply, number keys, arrow size; a `TaskCourse`'s `settings`, `TaskScene.Settings`; `TaskKit.DwellOf(step, settings)`), so two tasks in one project build differently; the kit's global `StandOff`, `PipStyle`, `AnchorPattern`, `PipAnchorPattern`, `WordsPerSecond`, `MinimumDwell`, `VoiceTail`, `WrongChoiceReply` and `NumberKeys` are removed. Which behaviors act on their targets (their highlights pulse, a Next waits for them, a teleport's spot keeps them in sight) is each behavior's declaration, as is every kind of row the task builds by (`TaskBehaviorAttribute.ActsOn`, `Offers`, `Advances`, `Holds`, `Places`; `TaskBehaviors.Traits`), so a project's own behavior joins them; a behavior puts its part on its step's page through `ITaskPageContent` (`TaskPage`: text, buttons, replies, key pad), as `placard`, `number_pad` and `option` now do, in place of the kit reading them by name. The arrow an `Indicator` makes has a `size` (`DefaultSize`), the same in metres however its target is scaled, and the placement search leaves it room by that size. The floor search starts past a part's own side, so a part larger than a person (a vehicle) has its floor found. The debug panel's tab and the task's command labels are fields (`DebugPanel.title`; `TaskDebug.skipLabel`, `backLabel`, `restartLabel`, `autopilotLabel`, which replace the constants), so a task says them in its own words.
  - Each placed spot is a `PlacedSpot` keyed by its step: a person moves it, Tools > unitytk > Placement > Capture Adjustments keeps it in a tuning file (relative to its part), and every build after puts it there (`TaskScene.TuningFile`). Viewpoints are placed before any other spot, so every build places alike; a second build changes nothing.

- **2026-11-19 -- New feature Panels: the task's placard, a UI Toolkit world-space panel a pointer and a headset's controller press alike; the Tasks kit builds one per task, a page per step, and the sheet's `placard`, `buttons` and `exit` rows (`Features/Panels` new: `Placard`, `PlacardPage`, `Placard.uxml`, `Panels.uss`, `Panels.tss`, `WorldPanel.asset`, actions `PanelsActions.MakePlacard`, `AddPage`, `SetUpInput`; Steps `StepChoice.finishes`, `Sequence.Pick`, actions `StepsActions.AddExit`, `ChooseOn` and a name-based `EnableDuring`; kit `TaskKit.PlacardName`, builders `ExitBehavior`, `PlacardBehavior`, `ButtonsBehavior`; the Tasks sample's on-screen readout becomes a debug bar).** UI Toolkit was weighed against a uGUI canvas (a second UI system) and a panel of 3D buttons (text and layout by hand): the XR Interaction Toolkit already presses world-space UI Toolkit panels from 3.2 (our pinned 3.3.1 has its public `XR UI Toolkit Manager`), so one panel serves desktop and VR with no package added. Its scene needs a `PanelInputConfiguration` that redirects no input through the EventSystem, which a desktop pointer works under too (`SetUpInput`); a world-space panel's render mode is serialized only (`PanelSettings.renderMode` is internal in 6000.3), so the panel settings ship as an asset made in the editor (a thousand panel pixels to the metre, a trigger collider over each document). The placard comes up in front of the user's eyes, to the left, each time a page shows, because viewpoints move the user from the cabinet's front to its back. A page per step is switched on by its step (the Viewpoints pattern), since a feature names no peer; the placard's choice reaches the sequence through Steps' own wiring (`ChooseOn`: a UnityEvent cannot call `Choose`, which returns whether the step offered it). An exit finishes the sequence wherever its step stands, which `Next` did only on the last step. Steps' `Wire` now names the method it wires when it looks for one already there (it looked for `Signal` alone, so a second `ChooseOn` would have wired again). `StepsActions.ChooseOn` and the new `EnableDuring` form take a name, as a dry run's placard and pages are only planned.
  - Test(s): `PlacardTests`, `PanelsActionsTests` (new: what it shows is on its document, a button per choice; a choice offered is raised, one not offered is not; it comes up in front of the eyes, facing them, turned with their heading alone; a page takes away only what it put there; the chain planned, one undo step, converging; a page for a missing placard raised); `SequenceTests` new `An_exit_finishes_the_sequence_wherever_its_step_stands_and_a_pick_chooses_for_an_event`; `StepsActionsTests` (the chain adds an exit and a choose wiring); `TaskSampleTests` (every row the sheet reads is built; the play-through presses each choice on the placard and exits).
- **2026-11-19 -- The Tasks sample: the kit's test task is a package sample at life size, and its demo scene, the one a person plays, is the one the kit's tests build and play through (`Samples~/Tasks` new: `task.csv`, `task.fbx`, `task.plan.json` moved from `Kits/Tasks/Tests/Task/`; `TaskDemoRecipe` (menu `Tools > unitytk > Samples > Tasks Demo`), `TaskDemo`, `DesktopLook`; `package.json` `samples`; `Kits/Tasks/Tests/TaskSampleTests.cs` replaces `CourseSceneTests.cs`; `tests/training_course.py` removed: the workspace's sample recipe `unity_samples/tasks.py` writes the task).** A task lived beside the tests, so nobody could open it and take it; and its parts were 10 cm boxes in the air, which tested the wiring but said nothing of how a task feels. Now the cabinet is 60 x 100 x 40 cm with a hinged door, two latches on its frame, a bolt, a lid and a button at its back, and a table carrying a key and a wrench. Each clip moves the cabinet alone and starts where the scene holds its part: Unity poses an imported model from its first take's start, so a tool clip that began in front of a latch left the key hanging there (found by looking at the built scene; the old task's clips all began at rest). The delivery now also states its record's `fps` (its `animation` record was refused for lacking it, so no shot list was written) and writes each box's normals (Unity warned and recalculated them). The demo recipe places it on a floor, builds its task, and puts a desktop rig in: a `Traveler` rig standing at the front view, its camera looking about with the right mouse button and clicking into the scene, a `Hand` in view, and an on-screen readout of the step with a button per choice, standing in for the placard. To play it with no project of your own, `unity_project.build key=play` makes one beside the owned test project (`docs/task.md`).
  - Test(s): `TaskSampleTests` (the task assesses with every node and take found; it builds as planned in one undo step; the demo scene stands the user at the cabinet, carries the rig, its tools resting on the table, and plays through as its sheet says); `tests/test_training_course.py` (reads the sample; the committed plan is what the reader makes of it); the workspace's `test_sync_unity_samples.py` (the recipe writes the committed files byte for byte).
- **2026-11-19 -- uGUI door: `PointerActions.SetUpPointer` makes the EventSystem a scene lacks, as Unity's GameObject > UI > Event System does (uGUI's `InputModuleComponentFactory`, so the module is the Input System's where the project reads input through it); `PointerActions.NoEventSystemCode` removed (the issue it named is gone).** A scene set up for clicks still needed a person to add the EventSystem by hand, and a recipe or agent had no call that did it.

  - Test(s): the package's compile: 6 errors (CS0012 and its cascade), then 0.
- **2026-11-19 -- Interaction's XRI door: every target made is selectable by a headset's controllers (`Features/Interaction/XRI` new door: `XRSelect`; `Hand.InScene`, the lookup both input doors use).** The door gives each target its meshes' colliders, the toolkit's `XRSimpleInteractable` (its hover, select and feedback are the toolkit's) and an `XRSelect`, the interactable's select wired to it as a persistent call the inspector shows; a select selects the target for the `Hand` on or above the controller that selected, else the scene's switched-on hand. Floor: the toolkit 3.3.1, as ArticulatedRig's door. Grab stays select-to-hand: the toolkit's grab interactable moves what it holds itself, which a `Holdable` does.
  - Test(s): `XRSelectTests` (new: a target made is selectable by a controller, planned once and undone; a controller's select reaches the hand on that controller): 483 pass in the owned 6000.3 project.
- **2026-11-19 -- Interaction: every target made is selectable by the project's inputs, through input doors that register as the editor loads; the uGUI door, a click through Unity's EventSystem (`Features/Interaction/Editor` new `ITargetInput` (internal), `Interactions.AddColliders`; `Features/Interaction/UGUI` new door: `PointerSelect`, `PointerActions.SetUpPointer`; `Holdable` switches its colliders off while held; `unity_compile.py` compiles doors on `com.unity.ugui`).** `Interactions.MakeTarget` wires each registered input after making the target (a door's editor half registers one from `[InitializeOnLoad]`, so the feature names no input type and the kit's builders change nothing): the uGUI door gives the target's meshes colliders and a `PointerSelect`, whose click (on the target or any collider under it, the EventSystem's bubbling) selects it for the hand named, else the scene's switched-on hand. `PointerActions.SetUpPointer(camera)` puts a `PhysicsRaycaster` on the camera and says when the scene has no EventSystem (Unity's own menu makes one with the input module the project uses). A held `Holdable` switches off its colliders (those that were on, back on as it goes back), so a tool in a desktop hand, in front of the camera, never catches the click aimed past it. `unity_package.compile` built no door on uGUI: its package list lacked `com.unity.ugui`, so the door was left out silently; it is listed now.
  - Test(s): `PointerSelectTests` (new: a target made is clickable, planned once and undone; a click on a mesh under it selects it for the scene's hand, the primary button alone; a camera set up, a missing EventSystem said); `InteractionsTests` new `A_target_made_is_wired_for_each_input_door_as_planned_and_once` (a stand-in door), `Each_mesh_under_a_node_gets_a_collider_planned_once_and_undone`, the chain's count taken with no door (`Interactions.OnlyInputs`); `HandTests` new `A_held_thing_switches_its_colliders_off_and_back_on_as_it_goes_back`.
- **2026-11-19 -- New feature Viewpoints, and the Tasks kit's `teleport` builder (`Features/Viewpoints` new `Traveler`, `TravelTo`, actions `ViewpointsActions.AddTraveler`, `AddTravelTo`; kit `TeleportBehavior`; `unitytk.sheets` the `teleport` template captures `targets`, so its phrase is matched to the delivery's nodes like any other row's and aliases reach it).** A viewpoint is a node of the delivery the user is brought to stand on, facing its +Z, each time its `TravelTo` is enabled (a step enables it as it starts). The `Traveler` is the user's body: on a rig (a headset's origin, a desktop rig) it turns the rig about its eyes and moves it, the floor put at the viewpoint's and the eyes keeping their height; on a camera that is its own eyes it stands `eyeHeight` over the viewpoint looking level. A `TravelTo` brings the one named, else the scene's switched-on one, so one scene serves whichever rig is on, and the build asks for no rig. No XR package is needed: a headset's origin is a rig like any other. The test task gains two viewpoints (`FRONT_VIEW`, `BACK_VIEW`) and a teleport in each section.

The task test found that two components on one object in one step (the key's target and its arrow) were planned as one change in a dry run: a step's enable now names the component's type, which the action takes as its type parameter, so it is known even for a part only planned. On a production sheet 14 of the plan's behaviors now build (171 of its 212 rows); the rest are the placard's (buttons, text, checklist, number pad, answers, quiz, exit), teleports and picture-in-picture.

- **2026-11-18 -- New feature Steps, and the Tasks kit builds a plan's steps (`Features/Steps` new `Sequence`, `Step`, `StepChoice`, `StepEvent`, actions `StepsActions.CreateSequence`, `AddStep`, `Await`, `SetAutoAdvance`, `AddChoice`, `EnableDuring`, `SignalOn`; Interaction `Target.Select` refuses while switched off, `ClipPlayer.Entry.then`, `Interactions.PlayAfter`; kit `TaskKit.Build` makes the sequence first, `SequenceName`, `TaskScene.Sequence`, new builders `advance`, `next`, `goto`, `select`, `play`; `scaffold_unity_part.py` names a plural feature's actions `<Feature>Actions`).** Steps run one at a time: the current one completes when every signal it awaits arrives (a clip's name as it ends, a selection's), goes on by itself or by a choice (Next, YES, Try Again), and enables the components it lists only while it runs, so a target is selectable in its own step alone. Neither feature names the other: a step enables Unity `Behaviour`s, and another part's event reaches the steps through `StepsActions.SignalOn`, which takes the event as Unity's own type (its owner any Unity object, an asset included). - **2026-11-18 -- New feature Interaction, and the Tasks kit's first builders (`Features/Interaction` new `Hand`, `Holdable`, `Target`, `ClipPlayer`, `HandEvent`, `ClipEvent`, actions `Interactions.AddHand`, `MakeHoldable`, `MakeTarget`, `AddClip`, `PlayOnSelect`, `TakeOnSelect`; `Kits/Tasks/Editor/Behaviors/` new `TakeBehavior`, `UseBehavior`, `HoldBehavior`; `ITaskBehavior.Apply` returns nothing; `unitytk.sheets` `SheetPlan.write(ctx=)`, `summary`, the reading pass now its internal base).** What a user acts on, with no input of its own (a pointer, a ray or code calls `Target.Select` with the hand it acts for), so one scene serves a desktop and a headset. A target accepts a selection by a hand holding what it requires; a holdable sits at a hand's anchor, rests at its home pose, and is lent to a clip that moves it, going back to the hand after; the clip player keeps every clip played in one Playables graph, each a layer over the ones before, so the scene stands where the last clip to move a thing left it. A selection's effect is a UnityEvent's persistent call, which the inspector shows. Each action takes the scene's objects, so a chain plans in a dry run what it then makes (a part an earlier step only planned counts as made). The builders compose it: `take` (holdable, target, taken on selection), `use` (a target requiring the row's tool, playing its clip), `hold` (the tool holdable).
  - Test(s): `Features/Interaction/Tests` (new: `HandTests`, `TargetTests`, `ClipPlayerTests`, `InteractionsTests`: the chain held to the kernel's three checks, the wiring played by a hand); `Kits/Tasks/Tests/UseBehaviorTests.cs` (new: a plan built into an imported delivery, played by a hand, held to the three checks); `tests/test_training_plan.py` 23 pass.
- **2026-11-18 -- New: the Tasks kit, a task built from a sequence sheet and its delivery, in two halves meeting at one file (`unitytk/task/` new `SheetPlan`, `PlanStep`, `PlanAction`, `PlanSource`, `ClauseBook`, `ClauseSpec`, `DeliveryIndex`, `NodeMatcher`, built-in clause templates `task/clauses/*.json`; package `Kits/Tasks/Editor` new `SheetPlan` (generated `SheetPlan.g.cs` + its reader), `TaskScene`, `TaskKit.Assess` / `Build`, `ITaskBehavior`, `TaskBehaviorAttribute`, `TaskBehaviors`, `TaskAssessment`; the `Kits/*` layers in `[tool.m3trik.layers.csharp]`; docs `docs/task.md`, `docs/parts/kit.md`).** The Python half reads a sheet through the Shot Manifest's parser (one parser for the DCC's shots and the task), each row by regex clause templates that are data (a project's own wording needs no code), and every phrase against the delivery's FBX nodes (`FbxFile`, no DCC) in ordered tiers that state a tie or a vaguer word instead of guessing and read a one-letter typo past, saying so. A step's clip rows name their takes as the manifest names its shots, and the hand is followed as the sheet runs, so a row used with what the hand holds names it as its tool. The plan is a typed `SchemaSpec`; its C# reader is generated. The Unity half assesses a plan against the delivery's instance (each action ready, unresolved or with no builder yet: the management view) and builds each ready action through its behavior's builder, found by attribute, so a behavior is added by a template and a builder, never by editing the loop. On a production sheet and its FBX (57 steps): 212 actions, 3 rows unread, all 28 takes matched to their rows, and 9 issues, each a node the delivery lacks, an alias to give, or a sheet typo. No builder is registered yet: the building blocks (Interaction, Steps, ...) are the next slices.

- **2026-11-12 -- Tests: the test folder is `tests/`, the pytest convention (was `test/`).** `test` is the standard library's own package name, so `from test.x import ...` could resolve to it. An editable install brings pythontk's own declared dependencies, so the job lists none of them.
- **2026-11-10 -- New feature Lods: one LODGroup per LOD group a delivery's `lods` record names (`Features/Lods/Editor/LodsImporter.cs`, the generated `LodsRecord.g.cs`; `Core/UnitytkRecords.cs` `Lods`; the package README's `lods` channel and Lods feature rows).** Unity's FBX import puts ONE LODGroup on a model's root, merging every `_LODn` mesh of the file by level index, and ignores the FBX's thresholds (measured, Unity 6000.3); the importer gives each group's node its own LODGroup at the heights the DCC set, and every LODGroup Unity made keeps only what no group claims (gone when that is nothing). Each level binds the way its group does: by exact name, else by namespace-stripped leaf (a tie is said and the level left out). A level the file lacks is said, the next one kept drawing its range and the last kept the group's cull; heights that miss a level are said and the group takes the default ladder (Unity refused the zeros it used to pad with). Without the record (or this package) Unity's own LODGroup stands: one asset per FBX works as before.
  - Test(s): `Features/Lods/Tests/LodsImporterTests.cs` (new: 9, all pass in the owned 6000.3 project); a Maya scene FBX with two groups imported with per-group LODGroups at 0.6/0.3/0 and 0.5/0.25/0 and no warning.
- **2026-11-08 -- Fix: `unity_package.compile` compiles the package's test assemblies too (`unitytk/unity_compile.py` new `tests` (default on), `TEST_FRAMEWORK`, `NUNIT`, `NETFX_SHIMS`, `_precompiled`; `operations.json`).** Every test assembly's constraints name the Test Framework, which the compile never defined, so all of them were left out: a green compile passed with a test file that did not compile (measured this round). Where the reference project holds the Test Framework, the editor build with the packages now defines `UNITY_INCLUDE_TESTS` and the framework's version define, and compiles the test assemblies against its `UnityEngine.TestRunner`/`UnityEditor.TestRunner`, NUnit from the project's `com.unity.ext.nunit` and the editor's .NET Framework facades NUnit needs. Without the Test Framework in the reference project they are left out and an info issue says so (`unity.compile.no_tests`; with no reference project, `unity.compile.no_packages` already does); `tests=False` is the faster loop. Cost measured on the shipped package: 37 s without the tests, 58 s with them (the variants run in parallel).
  - Test(s): `test_unity_compile.py` new `test_a_broken_test_file_fails_the_compile` (a temp copy whose `Kernel/Tests/ImportsTests.cs` does not compile: 0 errors with the tests left out, as before, 3 with them); `test_the_shipped_package_compiles` now bounds the run at 120 s.

The mask now takes its source image's format for the import's build target (`context.selectedBuildTarget`, which also reimports the model on a platform switch), in that family's alpha form: ASTC as it is, ETC as ETC2 RGBA8, DXT as DXT5, BC7 as it is; an uncompressed source leaves it uncompressed. A source's own platform override and its compression quality are carried (Unity's 0-100); with no source to follow, the editor's own compression at high quality, as before. A desktop target with Unity's default settings still gets DXT5, now at the source's quality (Normal by default) rather than always high. Android's module is not installed here; WebGL's ASTC subtarget stood in for a mobile GPU.

- **2026-11-08 -- `ProjectBuilder` builds a project of its own beside the owned one, and a sample's files can come from another delivery (`unitytk/project_builder.py` `folder(key=)`, `build(key=, replacements=)`, `import_samples(replacements=)`, new `NAME`, `KEY`).** A `key` (a short plain name) builds and refreshes `<NAME>-<key>` beside the owned project, so a run that must not share it -- the workspace's end-to-end suite, while the package's suites run -- has its own. `replacements` (`{sample: folder}`) delivers the folder's files in place of the sample's own of the same path, so the sample's tests check another producer's delivery of it; the stamp holds the files' digest, so a build without it imports the sample's own again, and a replacement naming no sample, or a file the sample does not hold, is refused before anything is copied.

So the importer scales every length it fills -- rest, anchor point, a slide's limits -- by that conversion x 100, read off the importer (1 for a centimetre file at the defaults): before, a metre file's rig snapped every joint to a hundredth of its place. Each root joint's chain now poses and grabs in its own space, the node its part hangs under (`ArticulatedRig.SpaceOf`, new), as every reader of the record does: the importer no longer refuses roots under different nodes (the rig goes on the nearest node above them all) nor two rigs on one node, so `ArticulatedRig` and `ArticulatedRigXRSetup` drop `[DisallowMultipleComponent]`; an off-chain part is placed in its parent joint part's frame (that part's own scale left out). `AddJoint` refuses a part another rig lists, and a root's rest is its local under its space. `ArticulationModel.Solve` has an overload solving into the caller's state on the model's own scratch (a warmed solve, pose and read allocate nothing; the rig's grab uses it), and the mirror is one table (`ArticulationModel.Mirror`, `MirrorFlips`, `MirrorLimits`, new) the importer and the tests share. The XR grab's `IsHolding` no longer counts a grab of a part above its own, so a hand that lost the rig steers and releases nothing. Set Limits From Pose's confirm lists the dry run's own narrowing changes (one predicate; its changes name `narrow` or `widen`). Slide values are labelled in the rig's units (metres for an unscaled space). Removed (no caller): `ArticulationModel.Joint.Slides`, `ArticulatedJoint.ChannelOf`, `ArticulatedRigImporter.Mirrored`, `ArticulatedRigActions.Narrowings`.
